## 2026-10-30 — bounded History/date/series-link discovery checkpoint

Latest outstanding user requirements: remove all request dates from History Status; add Requested and Updated columns with honest distinct event semantics. Compact dates everywhere in human dashboard: past 24h HH:mm, older YYYY-MM-DD; upcoming <=24h HH:mm, farther future YYYY-MM-DD. Full ISO/zone accessible in time/title; browser local enhancement and honest UTC server fallback. Preserve ISO machine API/CLI/report serialization and useful countdowns. Metadata history primary subject must be trustworthy retained series name; Links column on every actual table uses deduplicated positive verified AniDB IDs only, native accessible icon and fixed https://anidb.net/anime/{id}. No guessed associations or remote GETs. Preserve pagination, resizing, poll/disclosure behavior.

Read-only discovery findings: dashboard.py has one shared table renderer (all tracked groups, exclusions, history); widths keyed by column name v1, not numeric indices; no existing sorting or browser timezone formatter. History currently appends both ISO timestamps into Status. state._action_rows uses COALESCE(verified_iso,attempted_iso) for file update; send acceptance/rejection/uncertainty/void/operator-resolution times are not persisted per send. verified_iso records pending/unknown/partial/conflict inspection times too, not only success. Metadata updated_iso is durable; attempted_iso is pretransport fence, created_iso local reservation. prepare_metadata_operation ON CONFLICT replaces claim but does not reset created_iso, so replacement prepared claims inherit old creation time. SonarrSeries has title in memory; inventory cache persists episodes/files/fetched_at only, not series title; no source_json cache. Titles must be retained display-only by worker from exact series context, including per metadata claim, never inferred from IDs/path or current mapping. Existing sends.target_ids_json and frozen file_mapping_targets:v1:{file_id} keys contain proven AniDB IDs (keys encode tvdb/season/episode/absolute/anime/number/type); bounded safe display decoding needed. Unmapped scan exclusions lack anime evidence and keep blank Links.

Next bounded implementation: data/projection lane first. Add honest per-send updated timestamp migration and transition coverage with conservative legacy evidence; fix new metadata claim creation semantics; persist display-only title context from verified inventory/demand and expose bounded names/IDs without GET or write-authority coupling. Then sequential review. Shared UI lane after: one escaped <time> renderer plus browser-local formatter on initial render/polls, typed mapping-next-check fact instead of parsing diagnostics/prose, dynamic common columns and accessible AniDB link helper; History adds Requested/Updated and removes date strings from Status. Tests should cover lifecycle/legacy/archive/unknown/provenance, strict positive IDs/multi-target/bounds/redaction/no HTTP, all routes/time fields/boundaries/timezones/invalid dates and column counts. Browser fixture afterward; final all-lane delta sweep, dedicated full gate/package Docker remain outstanding. Three design choices for root: YYYY-MM-DD vs D MMM YYYY vs locale date (recommend YYYY-MM-DD); unknown title vs explicitly labelled folder fallback (recommend unknown); same-tab AniDB links vs safe new-tab rel=noopener noreferrer (recommend same-tab).

## 2026-10-29 — Renewed final L9 setup-view generation consistency fix (pending independent review)

Changed only service_setup.py and focused setup/settings tests, plus this authorized checkpoint. Selected the smallest of three improvements: bounded complete retry (implemented), splitting observation from snapshot construction, or explicit transactional observation receipts. The latter alternatives add more cross-cutting lifecycle machinery than this view needs.

## Metadata status P2 stale-generation/source write fence - implementation awaiting re-review

- Fixed independent review P2: a delayed old READY operation snapshot could replace a newer PREPARED generation's status row by anime, causing projection to hide the current entry. Added `StateStore.save_metadata_status_if_current`: inside the existing BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction it compares the exact previous status document, queries only the current operation claim, checks the currently saved Shoko source, and then replaces the document. No full operation decoding, schema changes, transport permission or retry rights are added.
- Both ordinary demand/recovery observation and manual-arrival projection now construct updates from the exact captured document. Competing writers cannot overwrite newer other-anime observations. None/no-operation observations cannot win after another handle creates a claim. Stale old-generation callbacks leave the newer document intact.
- Current source fencing uses the saved settings Shoko URL, or the legacy onboarding binding when that URL is not stored. An old-source observer is rejected even if it starts after reading an already-written new-source status document. Without durable endpoint evidence, the first source may establish a document but a different unknown source cannot displace it. Invalid current source values fail closed.
- Extracted the existing canonical source URL hashing into tiny shared `metadata_identity.py`; the existing endpoint-facing `metadata_operations.metadata_source_key` remains an unchanged public wrapper. This avoids duplicating normalization between recovery and persistence. Invalid raw URLs raise an opaque ValueError without exposing their content.
- Alternatives considered: key contexts by claim, use exact document CAS alone, or atomically check claim/document/current source together. Selected the third; claim-keyed contexts alone leave shared-document lost updates and document CAS alone misses old-source writers starting after a switch.
- Added real E1 accepted/READY -> E2 PREPARED -> delayed E1 READY regression, two-handle no-operation/new-claim race with exactly one full decode, competing other-anime document update, source switches both during and before a delayed old-source observation, and invalid source data. Existing PREPARED-before-HTTP, crash recovery, sanitizer and legacy projection defenses remain.

## Metadata status persistence/projection checkpoint - implementation awaiting independent review

- Assigned scope completed: bounded local metadata status data/persistence/projection only; no dashboard HTML layout, schema change, live writes, media operations, commit or push.
- Added `metadata_status.py`: latest 20 sanitized source-bound observations, actual supporting filename/Sonarr/Shoko paths and labeled IDs; exact operation claims fence context. Unclaimed pre-send context may bind only to the exact demand fingerprint. New generation context is persisted immediately after prepare, before possible HTTP. Changed saved Shoko URLs suppress old-server examples. Recovery without known file context never invents a file association.
- Scheduler captures fresh missing demands even when option OFF. Read-only recovery persists observation updates using the already-read operation snapshot (no extra ledger decoding against the recovery row-budget invariant). Manual metadata arrival settles unclaimed waits only from a freshly resolved complete file plan. Retained-operation no-ops preserve more useful recovery observations.
- `runtime.status().metadata` is `MetadataStatusView(enabled, message, examples)`; examples carry `anime_id`, optional `shoko_file_id`, `filename`, `sonarr_path`, `shoko_path`, `state`, static `message`, `observed_iso`, and optional `next_read_only_check_iso` (None because these paths do not establish an exact next check). All display reads are local. `cycle_report_payload` has separate metadata requests/deferred/observation fields, with no canary or verified-link credit.
- Known queue states remain combined queued/running/blocked because the existing scheduler/recovery observations do not carry finer-grained job state. Unknown queue access instructs admin queue permission checks; acceptance remains waiting, uncertain delivery never implies replay, durable READY alone establishes operation readiness. Latest observations are capped examples, not a complete current queue/count.
- Sanitization reuses `diagnostic_text` before truncation/persistence and again for legacy projection. Whole paths are sanitized before basename extraction so URL payloads cannot evade scrubbing after splitting. Version/source/claim/shape/document bounds reject malformed local status. Normal SERVICE first-hop missing-anime and second-hop internal-episode 404 become retryable WAITING/pending_metadata; first-hop does not create a LinkPlan. A known-ID second-hop wait retains its PendingTarget LinkPlan plus demand. Fresh passes rebuild complete ordinary LinkPlans when metadata arrives, even while optional maintenance is OFF. Advanced missing-anime skips/independent gates remain unchanged.

Contract: `MetadataDemand` freezes policy and credential-free current selected endpoint/root/path-mapping scope fingerprint; Sonarr series/file/path/size; Shoko file/size/canonical ED2K and ACTUAL Shoko absolute paths; complete immutable source episodes and mapped provenance; current typed missing needs; mapping target/structure digests, revision and override state. Whole-file `fingerprint` excludes missing/resolved progress and queue/timing state, so first-hop-to-second-hop progress remains stable. `MappedEpisodeTarget.identity`, `demand.required_targets`, and `demand.missing_targets` use `(anime ID, mappings.EpisodeType, episode number)`; needs deduplicate these identities while complete mapped targets preserve all source provenance. Internal daemon `_ReconcileResult.metadata_demands` is an immutable mapping keyed by Shoko file ID, with backward-compatible empty default. Integration must filter targets per anime before calling matching-anime state operations, recheck fresh consent/current scope, and use verified coverage when recording READY. A demand/scope fingerprint is NOT write authorization. This stage adds NO metadata HTTP mutations, reservation, canary credit, queue/scheduler integration, or ledger/schema changes.

Recovery/protections: legacy target_anime_not_cached AMBIGUOUS rows reopen only normal SERVICE and only via fresh exact source/path/size/hash and complete mappings; changed source, general ambiguous/conflicting, quarantined, campaign, prepared/sent/verified rows never gain metadata demand. Partial metadata waits are not pinned behind unrelated mapping refresh filters. Per-file resolution observations deduplicate identical mapping targets without persistent negative caching. A changed known AniDB episode ID is quarantined before exposing demand; pending counts derive from surviving plans/demands so quarantine does not leave a false wait count.

No metadata POST, persistent metadata operation integration, live private Shoko/Sonarr write, media operation, commit or push was performed by this implementer. The root owns that decision and later integration/review/validation gates. Non-actionable ignore list: EMPTY.

Queue adapter implementation (awaiting a DIFFERENT agent's read-only review): `queue_inventory.py` exposes a typed bounded inventory and PRESENT/ABSENT/UNKNOWN lookup for AniDB anime metadata and same-file ManualLink jobs across flag/episode/percentage variants. It reads Queue before/after positively paginated `Queue/Items` with `showAll=true`; preserves proven running identities on partial failure; and refuses absence on malformed/unknown relevant keys, forbidden/read failures, inconsistent/changing totals, duplicate keys, truncation, row/page/body limits or deadline. No human Title/Details guessing, raw keys, titles, exception text or secrets are returned. Existing aggregate `QueueSnapshot` callers are unchanged. `http.resolve_and_send` has one additive optional per-request timeout for a remaining cycle deadline; existing defaults and auth/redirect behavior remain unchanged. Body limits span all requests; identity encoding is requested and compressed replies are refused before decompression.

Three considered approaches: (1) dedicated typed read-only module, selected as the smallest complete separation from aggregate congestion checks; (2) add identities/paging to existing aggregate QueueSnapshot, useful capability but couples health checks to an admin-only inventory; (3) a generalized remote-job framework, rejected as needless complexity for the two existing job kinds. No speculative framework/dependency/schema was introduced.

Pinned upstream evidence was read from a local checkout whose actual HEAD is 892461188e5f1cc3ccf1b9b6eb1d4dfd31bea6f8 (Shoko 5.3.3), with QueueController also fetched publicly. `Scheduling/GenericJobBuilder/JobKeyBuilder.cs:154-200` serializes supplied writable fields as JSON into underscore-delimited keys; JobDataMapBuilder omits unchanged defaults. Verified primary forms: `AniDB.GetAniDBAnimeJob_AnimeID:18208` and `Import.ManualLinkJob_VideoLocalID:149_EpisodeID:650`, with metadata ForceRefresh/CacheOnly/DownloadRelations/RelDepth/CreateSeriesEntry/SkipTmdbUpdate variants. `QueueController.cs:112-151` requires admin for Items, accepts page>=1 and pageSize<=1000, and includes executing/waiting/blocked with showAll=true; pageSize=0 is unusable because Take(0). `ThreadPooledJobStore.cs:594-636` also prepends executing items in SQL-backed pages. Its lines684/709/738/760 set TotalTriggersCount = waiting + blocked + executing; summary TotalCount therefore includes running in actual implementation despite the model's waiting-only description. Queue/Types SQL counts WAITING only and is not absence evidence. `QuartzExtensions.cs:61-113` checks/schedules nonatomic jobs, so even a complete inventory cannot promise global exactly-once: another scheduler can race any snapshot or replace equal-size page contents. Future integration still requires a durable local operation ledger and fresh safety authorization.

### L12 pass 2: retained nonancestor boundary strands rewritten images (P2, open)

- Re-read AGENTS.md, PROJECT.md section 6, scoped brief, actual workflow/enumerator/test diff and current shared planner/registry safety paths. Root-supplied acceptance covers immutable event-SHA plan checkout, validated missing-before fallback, and shared necessary-build-input scan when the recorded boundary is absent; independent acceptance counted 91 targeted tests. These repairs preserve immutable registry gates and per-SHA validation. This status supersedes the original L12 pass 1 pending statements.
- New P2: scripts/enumerate_introduced.py:204-211 and :389-396 handle an available but nonancestor boundary inconsistently with an absent boundary. A tag or another branch can retain the old boundary after rewriting main before it. Missing-before push then enumerates all reachable pre-build history without the input filter, filling the cap with commits that cannot publish. Scheduled/default manual recovery returns no candidates because the retained boundary is not an ancestor. Valid replacement revisions remain stranded in overflow.

No project Git history or remote mutation.

### L1-ROOT-ALIAS (P0): filesystem-root aliases bypass explicit scope

## L3 P2 — saturating retry backoff (bounded implementation)

- Defect: both mapping refresh and daemon sleep computed `2.0 ** (failures - 1)` before capping. Count 1025 raises `OverflowError`; refresh error bookkeeping cannot advance the durable count/retry time.
- Considered three improvements: (1) independent threshold clamps at each site (small edits but repeats the saturation policy); (2) a shared capped-backoff helper bounding the integer exponent before scaling (selected); (3) iterative saturated doubling (readable but unnecessary loop work on every call). The selected helper uses `frexp` to derive the exact exponent bound and `ldexp` to retain existing power-of-two scaling. This avoids a magic clamp that changes timing for accepted positive fractional intervals.
- Repeated-pattern prevention: `src/shoko_linker/backoff.py:capped_backoff` now supplies both existing capped-exponential consumers. Mapping hours, daemon success-reset behavior, and jitter application/bounds are preserved.
- Changed by this bounded task: new `src/shoko_linker/backoff.py`; one import/call each in mapping_store.py and daemon.py; targeted cases in tests/test_mapping_store.py and tests/test_daemon.py. Other preexisting working-tree edits were preserved.
- Regression evidence: mocked HTTP500 after durable count 1024 now returns FAILED_TRANSPORT, persists count 1025 plus a 24-hour due time, retains active XML, and recovers the same retry scheduler after state.json loss. Function/daemon cases cover ordinary growth, cap transition, arbitrary huge counts, success reset, configured intervals above the cap, and jitter endpoints.

## L8 P1 atomic campaign drain authorization (implementation handoff)
- Finding: campaign.py inspected approval/send history before state.py acquired the grant transaction. Another StateStore could durably attempt file 501 after verified canary 500 was inspected, then the old unchecked UPDATE persisted a drain grant over an unsettled ledger. Concurrent pause/revoke, digest/approval replacement, drain revocation, and legacy ownership ambiguity had the same check/write gap.
- Options: (1) move approval/history validation and grant into one existing SQLite BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction (selected); (2) wrap the CLI in the apply OS lock (couples unrelated commands and does not fence arbitrary store handles); (3) ledger-generation CAS (requires unnecessary schema/version machinery). No new schema or broad abstraction.
- Changed src/shoko_linker/state.py: atomic grant validates current approval digest/approval time/bindings/drain snapshot, approved status, unresolved legacy ownership, and durable sends under the write lock. CampaignDrainRefusedError preserves refusal diagnostics. src/shoko_linker/campaign.py delegates, translates refusal to CampaignError, and logs verified settlement accurately. tests/test_campaign.py adds deterministic second-handle interleavings, a write-lock proof during history reads, and earlier-uncertainty regressions. Existing structured attempted_sends reporting remains intact.
- Settlement wording discrepancy: PROJECT says every attempted send VERIFIED while also allowing rejected/refused resubmission; existing F19 test explicitly retains rejected audit history after a verified retry. Per orchestrator direction, definite zero-write rejected/refused history is settled by later verified retry, but earlier attempted/ambiguous/partial/contradictory sends remain outstanding even when the file later has a VERIFIED row. PROJECT was not rewritten.
- Pattern/refactor rationale: this is the same validate-before-mutation TOCTOU family as atomic send authorization, so the guard now lives at the state mutation boundary with its grant, reusing the existing transaction primitive. Additional lock/version infrastructure would add complexity without improving this boundary.
- Validation (offline, targeted only): `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -o addopts='' tests/test_campaign.py -k 'f19 or drain or f10' tests/test_state.py -k 'f19 or drain or f10 or legacy_campaign_ownership' -q` => 58 passed, 302 deselected (24.30s). Targeted Ruff check passed, Ruff format --check reported 3 formatted files, targeted mypy passed for campaign.py/state.py/test_campaign.py. `git diff --check` passed (only Git CRLF normalization warnings). Initial system-python pytest could not import package; used configured .venv interpreter. Initial new-test context-manager mistake was corrected with closing() before passing validation.
- No full suite, live remote/media operations, credentials, or commits. Ready for a distinct read-only reviewer; no acceptance claim.

### L10 P2 implementation — pause-writes horizon validation
- Defect: `--hours=nan`, `inf`, or an enormous finite duration could reach pause horizon conversion after `store.pause`, raising an untyped exception after persisting the pause without its audit/horizon.
- Options: (1) strengthen existing shared numeric argparse validator and compute/catch the advisory horizon before mutation (selected); (2) catch failures after mutation (does not prevent partial state); (3) introduce duration value objects (unnecessary abstraction).
- Changes: cli.py requires finite positive numeric durations for its existing pause/attestation/campaign duration arguments; pause-writes computes its advisory timestamp and returns a ConfigError for unrepresentable timestamps before pausing. No automatic resume or expiry behavior added. Existing fixes preserved.
- Regression evidence: tests/test_cli_m7.py covers NaN/Inf/nonpositive CLI durations on all three argument consumers, no store construction on parser rejection, huge timedelta/datetime overflow and year-9999 overflow, untouched existing or absent pause/horizon/audit, and exact ordinary horizon plus audit persistence. tests/test_attestation.py retains no-lease/no-POST assertions while expecting earlier argparse SystemExit(2) for NaN/Inf.

First compatibility run found two assertions expecting handler return rather than parser exit; corrected to reflect earlier validation, then passed. Ruff check and format --check for the three affected Python files passed; scoped git diff --check passed. An initial new line length failure was corrected before these passing checks.
- Pattern assessment: the shared `_positive_number` validator already supplies the right prevention seam for all actual duration arguments; finite validation added there avoids duplicated guards. Timestamp representability remains a command-specific pre-mutation check. No broader refactor needed for this bounded defect. Offline only; no full validation, live operations, or commits. Await independent read-only review.

Valid duration/timezone arithmetic and lock ownership timing remain unchanged. No new timestamp framework, state interface, or unrelated consumers. Changed campaign.py, daemon.py, tests/test_campaign.py only; prior fixes preserved.
- Nine invalid lease cases cover nonpositive/nonfinite/huge/sub-resolution durations and near-datetime.max overflow through both entry points, asserting no meta lease, steal audit, or lock file. Valid fractional duration plus offset-aware clock regression verifies exact UTC expiry, original acquisition timestamp, owner, and release through both entry points.

### Resumed L6 P2 — hard safety observation masked by folder outage
- Reproduced six failing combinations: FileQualityFilterEnabled/MyList_AddFiles enabled while folder discovery is unavailable, has Unknown type, or has an unusable path. Previously these set only a temporary hold, so restoring settings/folders allowed old consent to resume.
- Options: duplicate an early raw-hard-setting check; always run shared service assessment and latch structured independent hazards before temporary-folder handling; add a new shared classifier. Chose shared existing assessment: smallest complete change, no duplicate policy or human-message parsing.
- Changed current_context to evaluate service safety even with incomplete folders and persist its independent unsafe_settings hold before transient handling. Nine regression cases cover the six hazards and three pure-outage controls, restart/recovery/resume, and clearing only through fresh consent. Folder-only outages with global renamers enabled still recover.
- Checks: red regression initially 6 failed/3 passed; focused outage/drift/recovery checks 18 passed; `.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest -q tests/test_service_setup.py tests/test_safety_check.py tests/test_onboarding.py` passed (84 collected); focused ruff lint and format checks passed; git diff --check passed (preexisting CRLF warnings). Initial bare python test attempt could not import installed project; used project venv thereafter. No full validation or live operations performed.
- Pattern decision: reusing existing structured hazard classification prevents repeated hard-setting lists. Separate actionable pattern remains: known nonhard settings drift during a folder outage is discarded because consent stores a combined settings/folder digest only. A temporary probe confirmed RenameOnImport True -> False with folders unavailable, restore True/folders, then resume authorized without fresh consent. Probe removed; reported to orchestrator for separate bounded fix with sanitized consent-setting observations, without fabricating folder facts.

### Resumed L6 P2 — independent observed settings drift during partial discovery
- Assigned follow-up preserves the separately reviewed/accepted hard-hazard precedence fix above. Reproduced the nonhard variant before implementation: RenameOnImport True -> False while folders were unavailable yielded only setup_unavailable and reconfirmation_required=False. First new regression failed at that durable-hold assertion (1 failed, 40 deselected, stopped immediately); restoring the original settings/folders could previously resume old consent.
- Three improving options considered: (1) persist the five sanitized boolean/unknown observations inside the already atomic digest-bound ScopeBinding and compare each known observation before partial-discovery handling; (2) persist an independent settings baseline in global metadata with explicit atomic consent-update coordination; (3) persist per-setting observation digests in the binding for partial comparisons. Chose (1): existing immutable domain boundary, readable facts, no additional metadata coordination or unnecessary per-value hashing. A combined digest alone cannot compare partial settings; synthetic old-folder contexts were rejected because they fabricate discovery facts.
- Changed onboarding.py and service_setup.py plus targeted tests only. ScopeBinding optionally serializes only the five known boolean/unknown safety values, validates exact allowed keys/types, and binds them into consent. Old documents still deserialize with their original digest and advanced OnboardingManager behavior; normal-service consent without an independent baseline requires fresh confirmation. New confirmation rejects an inconsistent baseline. No database schema/state.py change, raw Settings retention, version allowlist, endpoint or new physical-safety inference.
- Pattern prevention: one existing-controller boundary compares positive independent settings/version/capability/complete-root observations before any other unavailable discovery can short-circuit it. Known changes latch setup_changed; unknown observations do not become false or permanently latch a pure outage. Canonical capability comparison avoids input-order changes. Current_context is shared by startup/restart/resume/prewrite consumers; selected-folder combined digest still handles complete folder context. Previously accepted hard hazards retain unsafe_shoko_settings precedence.
